![](images/c974b9a7e34fbad5b4a5b84d6ad76af3da2f08574c73d62e05e6b587040021cd.jpg)  
Figure 2: The GRAIL pipeline. A signal is segmented into named regions; a codec encodes each as human-readable features and can regenerate it. In the game, a present feature keeps its observed value while an absent one triggers on-manifold regeneration, so ever<sub>y</sub> coalition is a <sub>p</sub>lausible real si<sub>g</sub>nal. Owen values (features nested in re<sub>g</sub>ions) are estimated exactl<sub>y</sub> with a common random-number sweep, yielding a complete, region-rolled-up attribution plus the residual ρ. An amortized network (blue), t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e same game, repro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>n one</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>pass</sub> f<sub>or</sub> f<sub>as</sub>t<sub>, ro</sub>b<sub>us</sub>t d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Our approach. We <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>ose GRAIL (Generative Re<sub>g</sub>ion Attribution with Inter<sub>p</sub>retable Labels), a framework that <sub>exp</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> ti<sub>me-ser</sub>i<sub>es</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> i<sub>n</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n-a</sub>li<sub>gne</sub>d <sub>un</sub>it<sub>s.</sub> GRAIL produces feature-based, additive explanations via a cooperative game: each region and each covariate is a player, <sub>an</sub>d it<sub>s va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> i<sub>s</sub> it<sub>s con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on across a</sub>ll <sub>coa</sub>liti<sub>ons,</sub> <sub>so</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>sum</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l’<sub>s</sub> <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>.</sub> S<sub>cor</sub>i<sub>ng a coa</sub>liti<sub>on requ</sub>i<sub>res say</sub>i<sub>ng w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l l<sub>oo</sub>k<sub>s</sub> like when a region is absent, but there is no canonical “of” <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>or a s</sub>t<sub>re</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> ti<sub>me ser</sub>i<sub>es suc</sub>h <sub>as an</sub> ECG<sub>: pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub> <sub>wor</sub>k fill<sub>s</sub> it <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>er</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l<sub>, a</sub> fl<sub>a</sub>t li<sub>ne, no</sub>i<sub>se, or</sub> <sub>a spec</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>ll<sub>y ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d <sub>surroga</sub>t<sub>e, an ar</sub>bit<sub>rary c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t f<sub>re-</sub> quently dominates the resulting explanation (Sundararajan and Najmi 2020). The choice of the absent state is thus not an implementation detail; it is the explanation. In this work, we stop choosing from a fixed menu and instead build the counterfactual: an interpretable codec encodes each region as named features, and a conditional generator regenerates an absent region on the data manifold, so every coalition <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>ns a p</sub>l<sub>aus</sub>ibl<sub>e rea</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>.</sub>

$$

With v(∅) defined, the Shapley/Owen axioms hold automati-<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>aggrega</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>y</sub> <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> <sub>an</sub>d<sub>,</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>an-</sub> nels (Section 4.3). Sohn, Lee, and Yan 2015) kee<sub>p</sub>s the anal<sub>y</sub>tic encoder but re<sub>p</sub>laces th<sub>e</sub> GP <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>er,</sub> i<sub>mprov</sub>i<sub>ng o</sub>f<sub>-man</sub>if<sub>o</sub>ld b<sub>e</sub>h<sub>av</sub>i<sub>or a</sub>t <sub>a sma</sub>ll on-manifold cost, and swa<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub> onl<sub>y</sub> the GP kernel (RBF, Matérn, or s<sub>p</sub>ectral) alters the counterfactual’s texture for robustness (A<sub>pp</sub>. D). The exact GP <sub>g</sub>ame is our on-manifold <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence:</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d<sub>-</sub>f<sub>orm</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>or</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t k<sub>eeps</sub> <sub>an</sub> <sub>exac</sub>t O<sub>wen sweep a</sub>f<sub>or</sub>d<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e, s</sub>i<sub>nce a</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>or pays a</sub> <sub>per-coa</sub>liti<sub>on samp</sub>li<sub>ng cos</sub>t it <sub>canno</sub>t <sub>amor</sub>ti<sub>ze.</sub>

O<sub>we</sub>n <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ues a</sub>nd <sub>exac</sub>t <sub>co</sub>m<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub>n<sub>ess.</sub> W<sub>e a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea-</sub> t<sub>ure</sub> i<sub>mpor</sub>t<sub>ance w</sub>ith O<sub>wen va</sub>l<sub>ues,</sub> th<sub>e average marg</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>con-</sub> t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>on-</sub>f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>permu</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>preserve</sub> the block (re<sub>g</sub>ion) structure. Since features are nested in re-<sub>g</sub>i<sub>ons,</sub> th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r</sub> O<sub>wen</sub> <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ues</sub> <sub>ro</sub>ll <sub>up</sub> <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance,</sub> values of the QRS re<sub>g</sub>ion’s R-amplitude and width should sum to the QRS’s Sha<sub>p</sub>le<sub>y</sub> value (Owen consistenc<sub>y</sub>), so the “re<sub>g</sub>ions → features” ex<sub>p</sub>lanation is coherent. A telesco<sub>p</sub>- in<sub>g</sub> common-random-number (CRN) swee<sub>p</sub> estimates ever<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub><sup>l</sup>a<sub>y</sub>er <sup>f</sup>rom $| N | { + } 1$ <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>s a</sub>l<sub>ong one nes</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n:</sub>

T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 1<sub>:</sub> C<sub>o</sub>d<sub>ec</sub> <sub>zoo:</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h i<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erpre</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>enco</sub>d<sub>er</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d with a conditional generator. All are general except clinical (ECG); shape is the default.

$$
\phi _ { i } ~ + = ~ f ( x ^ { \prime } ( S \cup \{ i \} , \omega ) ) - f ( x ^ { \prime } ( S , \omega ) ) .\tag{3}
$$

## 4 E<sub>xper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l E<sub>va</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>

W<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> GRAIL <sub>on s</sub>i<sub>x</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>s across</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>ns:</sub> t<sub>wo syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> f<sub>am</sub>ili<sub>es w</sub>ith <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>on mas</sub>k<sub>s</sub> (Synth and Mixed, which add static covariates); two clinical ECG families with cardiolo<sub>g</sub>ist P/QRS/T annotations (ECG, from MIT-BIH (Mood<sub>y</sub> and Mark 2001) and QT (La-<sub>g</sub>una et al. 1997); and multivariate LUDB (Kal<sub>y</sub>akulina et al. 2021), carr<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g p</sub>atient a<sub>g</sub>e and sex; and two industrial-sensor families without re<sub>g</sub>ion annotation (FordA, Wafer). All tar-<sub>g</sub>ets are Ince<sub>p</sub>tionTime (Ismail Fawaz et al. 2020) classifiers.

We invoke GRAIL in three configurations: (i) provided regions; (ii) discovered regions with GRAIL-auto; and (iii) amortized deployment with GRAIL-fast, against six ti<sub>me ser</sub>i<sub>es-spec</sub>ifi<sub>c w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>per</sub>t<sub>ur</sub>b<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes</sub> (WindowSHAP (Na<sub>y</sub>ebi et al. 2023), TimeSHAP (Bento et al. 2021), TS-MULE (Schle<sub>g</sub>el et al. 2021), LIMESe<sub>g</sub>- ment (Sivill and Flach 2022), D<sub>y</sub>namask (Crabbé and Van Der Schaar 2021)), the closest conce<sub>p</sub>t- and <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>-level Sha<sub>p</sub>le<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>ers (C-SHAP (Jutte et al. 2025), GS-SHAP (Kim and Park 2026)), and non-time series <sub>g</sub>radient and sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> attributions (Inte<sub>g</sub>rated Gradients (Sundararajan, Tal<sub>y</sub>, and Yan 2017), <sub>g</sub>radient×in<sub>p</sub>ut (Simon<sub>y</sub>an, Vedaldi, and Zisserman 2014), KernelSHAP (Villani, Lockhart, and Ma<sub>g</sub>azzeni 2022)).

## 4<sub>.</sub>3 T<sub>e</sub>m<sub>po</sub>r<sub>a</sub>l Si<sub>g</sub>n<sub>a</sub>l <sub>a</sub>nd C<sub>ova</sub>ri<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es o</sub>n On<sub>e</sub> S<sub>ca</sub>l<sub>e</sub>

Th<sub>e coopera</sub>ti<sub>ve game a</sub>d<sub>m</sub>it<sub>s s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>c covar</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es as s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>on</sub> <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>ers (Section 3.1), so si<sub>g</sub>nal re<sub>g</sub>ions and metadata share <sub>a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e sca</sub>l<sub>e, an</sub>d <sub>a mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l’<sub>s re</sub>li<sub>ance on</sub> it i<sub>s rea</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erre</sub>d<sub>.</sub> O<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> LVH t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>sex</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>age</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>ayers</sub> at matched instances, region support, and budget (n=40), a<sub>g</sub>e rece<sup>i</sup>ves mean $\vert \phi \vert = 1 . 6 6 3$ <sub>, a</sub>b<sub>ove</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>op</sub> ECG r<sub>eg</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n (1.542; QRS 1.430), while sex, a near-distractor, receives 0.238; together the statics carry 24.8% of the total |ϕ|, and age is the highest-ranked player on 62.5% of instances. Admitting the statics costs no on-manifold faithfulness (0.598 to 0.597, p=0.89) and leaves completeness exact, but it drops the expected-region hit rate from 0.575 to 0.325 (p=0.002): QRS loses top rank to a<sub>g</sub>e exactl<sub>y</sub> where the model leans <sub>on age.</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> I <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e per-p</sub>l<sub>ayer</sub> t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e, a syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>or con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>, an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>u</sub>ll <sub>a</sub>bl<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Th<sub>e same cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o mu</sub>lti<sub>c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> i<sub>n</sub> which the <sub>g</sub>ame <sub>p</sub>laces ever<sub>y</sub> lead on a sin<sub>g</sub>le scale (Section 3.1). We run the LVH task on LUDB twice under an <sub>o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se</sub> id<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>on, once on</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>s</sub> I/II/V1 <sub>an</sub>d <sub>once on</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>d II <sub>a</sub>l<sub>one, a</sub>t th<sub>e same re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d O<sub>wen</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t $( P { = } 4 8 , \ Q { = } 2 )$ , the same n=20 instances and seeds, and both re<sub>g</sub>ion re<sub>g</sub>imes (Table 3). Three leads beat one on ever<sub>y</sub> qualit<sub>y</sub> metric in both re<sub>g</sub>imes (on-manifold faithfulness 0.659 to 0.735 with provided regions, 0.454 to 0.511 with discovered ones) at about 39× the runtime (2.4 to 94.4 s). ## A R<sub>e</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d W<sub>or</sub>k

Sh<sub>ap</sub>l<sub>ey</sub> <sub>a</sub>ttrib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n <sub>a</sub>nd th<sub>e</sub> r<sub>e</sub>m<sub>ova</sub>l <sub>ope</sub>r<sub>a</sub>t<sub>o</sub>r<sub>.</sub> Sh<sub>ap</sub>l<sub>ey</sub> values, ori<sub>g</sub>inall<sub>y p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>osed b<sub>y</sub> Sha<sub>p</sub>le<sub>y</sub> (1953), form the foundation of the modern attribution framework SHAP (Lundber<sub>g</sub> and Lee 2017). The framework satisfies four basic axi<sub>oms: e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>ency, symme</sub>t<sub>ry,</sub> d<sub>ummy, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>v</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> Th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ons o</sub>f Sh<sub>ap</sub>l<sub>ey a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons are</sub> th<sub>e</sub> “<sub>a</sub>b<sub>sence</sub>” and samplin<sub>g</sub> of features (<sup>Š</sup>trumbelj and Kononenko 2014). Building on top of the framework, Sundararajan and Najmi (2020) and Kumar et al. (2020) introduce attribution ambi-<sub>g</sub>uities; Janzin<sub>g</sub>, Minorics, and Blöbaum (2020) re<sub>p</sub>resent th<sub>e o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l<sub>-vs-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>c</sub>h<sub>o</sub>i<sub>ce as causa</sub>l<sub>;</sub> C<sub>over</sub>t<sub>,</sub> Lundber<sub>g</sub>, and Lee (2021) <sub>p</sub>arameterize removal-based ex-<sub>p</sub>l<sub>ana</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a</sub> <sub>mas</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> <sub>opera</sub>t<sub>or.</sub> T<sub>o</sub> <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>remova</sub>l <sub>mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm</sub> f<sub>or</sub> ti<sub>me</sub> <sub>ser</sub>i<sub>es,</sub> <sub>we</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>remova</sub>l <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a con</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>or</sub> t<sub>o s</sub>t<sub>ay on-man</sub>if<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>.</sub> F<sub>ur</sub>th<sub>er, we</sub> <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d th<sub>e va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>rom a</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l Sh<sub>ap</sub>l<sub>ey game</sub> to a two-level Owen value (Owen 1972) over nested features <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>correspon</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> ti<sub>me-ser</sub>i<sub>es reg</sub>i<sub>ons.</sub>

The encoder is a <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d<sub>-</sub>f<sub>orm rea</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e reg</sub>i<sub>on w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t fitti<sub>ng an</sub>d l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers.</sub> Th<sub>us,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>mean-</sub> in<sub>g</sub> across all re<sub>g</sub>ions and si<sub>g</sub>nals. stat encodes level (re<sub>g</sub>ion mean), slope (least-squares linear coeficient in t), and scale (standard deviation of the residual after removin<sub>g</sub> level and slo<sub>p</sub>e). shape is a strict su<sub>p</sub>erset that adds a named extremum: peak\_amp, the si<sub>g</sub>ned hei<sub>g</sub>ht of the dominant extremum above the fitted line, and peak\_loc, its <sub>p</sub>osition as a fraction of the region span in [0, 1] normalized in a <sub>way</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons o</sub>f dif<sub>eren</sub>t l<sub>eng</sub>th<sub>s poo</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o one re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> distribution. shape’s scale is calibrated on the residual <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fitt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pea</sub>k<sub>, so</sub> th<sub>e genera</sub>t<sub>or</sub>’<sub>s var</sub>i<sub>ance ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t the named features leave behind. basis encodes low-order DCT coeficients; sm encodes s<sub>p</sub>ectral-mixture h<sub>yp</sub>er<sub>p</sub>arameters; nonstationary encodes a <sub>p</sub>er-re<sub>g</sub>ion len<sub>g</sub>thscale <sub>p</sub>rofile. Under GRAIL\_stabilized, the slo<sub>p</sub>e is a Theil– S<sub>en es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f l<sub>eas</sub>t <sub>squares.</sub> Th<sub>e genera</sub>t<sub>or</sub>’<sub>s mean</sub> is the encoder read backwards (level + slope · (t − t<sup>¯</sup>), plus p<sup>eak</sup>\_<sup>am</sup>p $\cdot \ g ( t )$ for shape, with g a Gaussian bump of width max(1.5, 0.08L) at peak\_loc · (L−1)); the residual is <sub>a</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary-con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d GP d<sub>raw.</sub> All <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ere use</sub> shape with an RBF residual kernel.

G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an</sub> P<sub>rocess.</sub> Th<sub>e res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l k<sub>erne</sub>l h<sub>as an amp</sub>lit<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e,</sub> fixed by the encoded scale, and a lengthscale $\ell = f \cdot L$ f<sub>or</sub> region length L. Synth is a synthetic family of T=100-step univariate series: i.i.d. background with localized injected features whose combinations define the target classes, and the injection <sub>mas</sub>k<sub>s are</sub> th<sub>e groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>on a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> D<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a s</sub>i<sub>ze:</sub> 1500×1×100.

Mi<sub>xe</sub>d <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>d<sub>s</sub> S<sub>y</sub>nth <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures ac</sub>ti<sub>ng as s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>-</sub> <sub>cu</sub>t<sub>,</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>ve,</sub> <sub>or</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>or</sub> <sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>es,</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d setting with known signal and known covariate ground truth. Data size: 1500×1×100.

ECG uses the MIT-BIH Arrh<sub>y</sub>thmia (Mood<sub>y</sub> and Mark 2001) and QT (La<sub>g</sub>una et al. 1997) databases from Ph<sub>y</sub>sioNet (Goldber<sub>g</sub>er et al. 2000); each R-ali<sub>g</sub>ned beat carries cardiolo<sub>g</sub>ist P/QRS/T re<sub>g</sub>ions across five binar<sub>y</sub> tasks. Data size: 4000×1×288.

Target models. All ex<sub>p</sub>lanations tar<sub>g</sub>et Ince<sub>p</sub>tionTime (Ismail Fawaz et al. 2020) classifiers trained <sub>p</sub>er dataset. We t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>w</sub>ith Ad<sub>am a</sub>t <sub>a pea</sub>k l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng ra</sub>t<sub>e o</sub>f $1 0 ^ { - 3 }$ and a batch size of 64. For the synth, ecg, forda, and wafer families, utilize the fastai one-c<sub>y</sub>cle schedule $( \mathtt { f i t \_ o n e \_ c y c l e , l r _ { \mathrm { m a x } } } = 1 0 ^ { - 3 } )$ <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>a va</sub>lid<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h size of 128 and a TSStandardize batch transform. The static-fusion families (mixed, ludb, and the 3-lead ludb build) use a <sub>p</sub>lain torch loo<sub>p</sub> at a constant $1 0 ^ { - 3 }$ <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>no sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>e.</sub> E<sub>very</sub> f<sub>am</sub>il<sub>y m</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>zes cross-en</sub>t<sub>ropy.</sub> E<sub>poc</sub>h <sub>num</sub>b<sub>ers are</sub> 50 on synth and mixed, 30 on ecg, 40 on forda and wafer, and 60 on both ludb builds.

Methods and configurations. GRAIL uses the shape codec on general families and the clinical codec on ECG, a class-conditional (normal-cohort) reference, and the lossless <sub>morp</sub>h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>ogy-res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>p</sub>l<sub>ayer.</sub> It <sub>appears</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>ree con</sub>fi<sub>gura-</sub> tions: with a <sub>p</sub>rovided re<sub>g</sub>ion <sub>p</sub>rior (GRAIL); discoverin<sub>g</sub> its <sub>own</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>-aware</sub> <sub>segmen</sub>t<sub>er</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> sna<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub> (GRAIL-auto, §3.2); and as the amortized sin<sub>g</sub>leforward-<sub>p</sub>ass de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment (GRAIL-fast, §3.3). The <sub>p</sub>erturbation and windowin<sub>g</sub> baselines are WindowSHAP (stationar<sub>y</sub>, d<sub>y</sub>namic) (Na<sub>y</sub>ebi et al. 2023), TimeSHAP (Bento et al. 2021), TS-MULE (uniform, SAX) (Schle<sub>g</sub>el et al. 2021), LIMESe<sub>g</sub>ment (Sivill and Flach 2022), TsSHAP (Ra<sub>y</sub>kar et al. 2023), and D<sub>y</sub>namask (Crabbé and Van Der Schaar 2021). C-SHAP (Jutte et al. 2025) and GS-SHAP (Kim and Park 2026) do not release code. We have reim<sub>p</sub>lemented th<sub>em an</sub>d <sub>ma</sub>d<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e co</sub>d<sub>e ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e supp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>ary</sub> materials. Gradient baselines are inte<sub>g</sub>rated <sub>g</sub>radients (Sundararajan, Taly, and Yan 2017), gradient×input, and KernelSHAP (Lundber<sub>g</sub> and Lee 2017). The full head-to-head <sub>uses</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>amor</sub>ti<sub>ze</sub>d<sub>-arm</sub> t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>es</sub> <sub>use</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>rep-</sub> resentative four-baseline subset (one <sub>p</sub>er <sub>p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>m) at the <sub>re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ze</sub> $n _ { \mathrm { a m o } } .$ Th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> th<sub>e same re-</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>on suppor</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d h<sub>arness;</sub> GRAIL<sub>-auto</sub> di<sub>scovers</sub> it<sub>s own.</sub>

Metrics. Faithfulness is scored under two matched removal operators, a neutral of-manifold splice and an on-manifold re<sub>g</sub>eneration, via com<sub>p</sub>rehensiveness/suficienc<sub>y</sub> (DeYoun<sub>g</sub> et al. 2020), faithfulness correlation (Bhatt, Weller, and Moura 2020), and deletion/insertion $\mathrm { { A U C } ; }$ th<sub>e on- an</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f<sub>-</sub> manifold variants are re<sub>p</sub>orted side b<sub>y</sub> side (the $_ { - } \mathrm { o n } / \mathrm { \_ o f f }$ columns). Localization compares the per-timestep saliency to the annotated re<sub>g</sub>ion mask (Ismail et al. 2020) b<sub>y</sub> AUPRC and a pointing game. Robustness covers shift/jitter stability, maxsensitivit<sub>y</sub>, and the model-randomization sanit<sub>y</sub> check (Adebayo et al. 2018). Cost is the efective-player count and wallclock runtime; completeness is ${ \overline { { | \rho | } } } ;$ ; and self-segmentation is b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>ary</sub> $F _ { 1 }$ <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mas</sub>k<sub>.</sub>

St<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>p</sub>r<sub>o</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>.</sub> Withi<sub>n</sub> <sub>a</sub> f<sub>am</sub>il<sub>y,</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d i<sub>s</sub> <sub>score</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances</sub> f<sub>rom a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e run, so a</sub>ll <sub>com-</sub> <sub>par</sub>i<sub>sons are genu</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>l<sub>y pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d<sub>.</sub> E<sub>very</sub> “<sub>w</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>/l<sub>oses</sub>” <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>m</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d t<sub>wo-s</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d Wil<sub>coxon</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gne</sub>d<sub>-ran</sub>k t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>on</sub> <sub>per-</sub> instance diferences, corrected family-wide with Benjamini– H<sub>oc</sub>hb<sub>erg</sub> <sub>a</sub>t $q < 0 . 0 5$ <sub>, an</sub>d <sub>repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ze.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>samp</sub>l<sub>e s</sub>i<sub>zes are prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 2<sub>.</sub>

## J.1 Amortizin<sub>g</sub> the <sub>g</sub>ame for de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ment
